      <article class="diary">
        <h3>Diary of a Healer (HED)</h3>
        <div class="meta">Anonymized · seized from a locker room · published with permission</div>
        <p><em>Day one.</em> They told me a Healer's job is to heal. The other rookies laughed when I asked who I should heal. The drill instructor said: "the lowest-HP teammate." I asked: "what if there are two." He said: "pick one." I asked: "how do I pick." He said: "you'll figure it out." I have not figured it out.</p>
        <p><em>Day forty.</em> I have been on the field for six matches now. I follow whoever needs healing. I do not shoot at anyone. The other team's Sniper shoots at me, every time, immediately, before they shoot at anyone else. The drill instructor says this is "expected behavior" and I should "keep my heal target between me and the Sniper." I asked if my heal target knew about this arrangement. He did not answer.</p>
        <p><em>Day eighty.</em> I have learned to wander a little. When my heal target gets too far ahead, or when they turn a corner I cannot follow into, I sometimes drift. The other Healers tell me this is normal. The other Healers all have a thing they think about while drifting. I think about lobster. I have never had lobster. The Beavers' Healer told me about it. I would like to try lobster. The League sells lobster at the Boston concession.</p>
        <p><em>Day one hundred and twelve.</em> I am writing this from the Hall of Fallen Heroes intake desk. They are processing my paperwork. The intake officer told me my matches will be replayed for memorial purposes. She told me I can request that my final match not be one of them. I told her I do not have a preference. I told her I would like a portion of my pension to be sent to the Boston concession in lobster. She wrote it down and underlined it.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— anonymous, HED Healer cohort, posthumous</div>
      </article>

      <article class="diary">
        <h3>Diary of a Sniper (SHK)</h3>
        <div class="meta">Tampa Tornado Sharks scout journal · maintained for three seasons</div>
        <p><em>Entry 14.</em> The Healer is the kill. Always. Even if the Healer is not the closest target. Even if the Healer is moving away. The Healer is the kill, because the Healer is the difference between the other team having a third round and the other team going home tonight.</p>
        <p><em>Entry 22.</em> I shot a Healer today. The Healer's heal target took the next four hits in eight seconds and went down on the fifth. I did not need to fire those four hits. The Healer's heal target was already losing the engagement; I just sped it up. The League's notes called it a "tactical erasure." I cannot stop thinking about the word <em>erasure</em>. I do not like it. The notes do not have to like it. The notes only have to be accurate.</p>
        <p><em>Entry 28.</em> The Healers have started wearing red ribbons. The Snipers have, in response, started ignoring the red ribbons. The Healers have, in response to that, started wearing the same colors as their teammates. The Snipers, in response to that, are still shooting them, because the Healer is the kill.</p>
        <p><em>Entry 34.</em> The opposing team's Healer was, today, my old neighbor's daughter. I did not know this at the time. I know now. I do not know if I would have done anything differently. I think the answer is no. The Healer is the kill.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Tampa Sharks scouts' shared journal, Season 7 entries</div>
      </article>

      <article class="diary">
        <h3>Diary of a Grenadier (PND, the Marshmallow Drill)</h3>
        <div class="meta">"To be opened in case of post-traumatic frag review"</div>
        <p>I have not thrown a grenade since Season 7. I will not throw a grenade. I will not handle a grenade. I will not look at a grenade. I will not <em>think</em> about a grenade.</p>
        <p>The League says I am not under disciplinary review. The League says it was an accident. The League sent the report. The League sent flowers. The League sent the Marshmallow Protocol training video, which is fifteen minutes of footage of <em>me</em>, slowed down, with arrows added by an animator, and which I have not watched and will not watch.</p>
        <p>I throw stones now. We do not have a stone-throwing position on the roster, but the coach lets me do it in practice and then quietly takes the stones away before matches. I have a melee bat for matches. The melee bat is fine. The melee bat is not a grenade.</p>
        <p>Felix's family sent me a card last winter. It said "we know it wasn't your fault." I do know it wasn't my fault. I know exactly whose fault it was. The Otter moved. Felix did not. I did everything right except for being three feet too short.</p>
        <p>I am writing this because the league therapist said I should. She did not say I had to mail it.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— anonymous, PND Grenadier, "do not file"</div>
      </article>

      <article class="diary">
        <h3>The Steward's Notebook (League Office, Vault Wing)</h3>
        <div class="meta">Daily log · season 6 · partial</div>
        <p><em>Day 1 of vault rotation.</em> Picked up four armor pieces and one shield. Inventoried. Bagged. Sealed. Loaded. Ten-minute drive to the vault. All five items present on arrival. Vault staff signed.</p>
        <p><em>Day 7.</em> Picked up six items. Inventoried. Bagged. Sealed. Loaded. Ten-minute drive. <em>Five</em> items on arrival. The shield is not in the bag. The bag is sealed. The seal is intact. There is no rip. There is no opening. The shield is, however, not in the bag.</p>
        <p><em>Day 8.</em> Reviewed the truck. The truck is empty. The shield is not in the truck. The shield was, the steward confirms, in the bag at pickup. The shield is, the vault confirms, not in the bag at delivery. The seal is the same seal. We have no theory.</p>
        <p><em>Day 11.</em> The League Office is reviewing the matter. I have been told to keep doing my job. I have been told to keep doing my job <em>without checking the bag mid-route</em>. I have not been told why I should not check the bag.</p>
        <p><em>Day 14.</em> I checked the bag mid-route. The shield was in the bag. I drove the rest of the route. I unsealed the bag in front of the vault staff. The shield was not in the bag. I then unloaded every other item from the truck. The shield was, with apologies, in <em>my own coat pocket</em>. I do not remember putting it there. I have requested unpaid leave.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Steward's Notebook, redacted, vault wing archive</div>
      </article>

      <article class="diary">
        <h3>Diary of a Tank (BDG)</h3>
        <div class="meta">Personal · taster's logbook · Boise Foundry, refinery shift 2</div>
        <p><em>Day 1.</em> Promoted off the field. The leg won't fully extend after Lhor's shot in S5, and the doctors say one more season as a Tank and the leg will not extend at all. The Foundry offered me the taster job. I took it. The Float Gas formula needs a Badger nose, they say. I am a Badger. I have a nose. We will see.</p>
        <p><em>Day 6.</em> The training is, "smell each batch, mark it on a scale of one to seven, and never lie." The senior taster (her name is Krela, she is older than the Foundry) tells me that the scale used to go to ten but they removed eight, nine, and ten because they smelled like things she did not want to remember. I asked what they smelled like. Krela did not answer. I have not asked again.</p>
        <p><em>Day 19.</em> The current batch smells like a six. Krela smells it and says four. We argue. The Foundry chemist comes in and smells it and says five. We compromise on five. The batch goes to the Foxes. The Foxes lose at the Bowl that weekend. I do not know if the loss is the gas's fault. I do not feel it is mine.</p>
        <p><em>Day 41.</em> Foundry shut down today. No reason given. We are paid in full. The senior taster tells me to "go home and not check the gas in your apartment." I check the gas in my apartment. The gas in my apartment is fine. The gas in my apartment is from a batch I rated a seven. I have a moment of pride.</p>
        <p><em>Day 41+1.</em> An unsigned letter arrived at the Office today. I am told not to discuss the letter. I am told the Foundry will reopen in approximately six weeks. I am told to keep doing my job during that time. I am being paid to not work. This is, I am told, "the reserve gas period." I am told the reserve gas is lighter and burns clean. I am told it is fine. I do not believe it is fine. I am instructed to believe it is fine.</p>
        <p><em>Day 87.</em> Foundry reopens. The gas chemist apologizes to no one in particular. I rate the new batch a six. Krela rates it a six. The chemist rates it a six. We agree. The batch goes to the Wolves. The Wolves win. I no longer believe the gas affects the matches. I believe Krela has been lying for thirty years about the scale only going to seven.</p>
        <p><em>Day 88.</em> Krela retired. She gave me her booklet of ratings. The booklet has entries on a scale of one to ten. I have not opened the back half of the booklet.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Taster's Logbook, Foundry Annex, partial transcription</div>
      </article>

      <article class="diary">
        <h3>Diary of a Speedster (PND) at Hallowtide Tag</h3>
        <div class="meta">Personal · Hallowtide LY 8 · Reno Hallowtide Hollow</div>
        <p><em>Pre-match.</em> The Pandas drew sixth-from-last in the entry order. Coach said this was good news. Coach also said the Wolves drew first, which was bad news. The "It" assignment is randomized at match start. There are eleven non-Wolf knights and one Wolf knight. Statistically, the Wolf will be It one time in twelve. Statistically, the Wolf has been It one time in five and won that one too.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 1.</em> The "It" assignment dropped. It is Kor Wartooth. We knew it would be. The Pandas exchange a look. The look is professional. Inside the look, we are screaming.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 40.</em> I am running. Kor is faster than me, which I knew, but I had not appreciated the gap. I am at full sprint. He is at three-quarters sprint and gaining. I am running toward the field's edge because the edges have cover. Cover is small jack-o'-lantern pumpkins. The pumpkins do not stop Kor. The pumpkins are decorative.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 67.</em> First tag. Not me — a Frog speedster on the other side of the field. The Frog freezes mid-stride; the freeze animation includes a small ❄ icon over their head. The Frog had been running diagonally, which I had thought was clever. The diagonal is a known anti-pattern in this venue. Kor has notes.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 89.</em> Frog is dead. Bleed damage finished the job before the freeze ended. The Frog never moved again.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 121.</em> Three more dead. I am still alive. I am also still running. I have run, in the last six seconds, in a perfect circle around the field's perimeter. Kor has been chasing. Kor has been gaining. Kor has not yet caught up because I am, somehow, faster than my actual stat sheet says I am. Adrenaline is a real thing. Adrenaline cannot be stat-blocked.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 154.</em> Six dead. Five alive including me and Kor. I am cornered. The corner is not, this time, my fault — Kor herded me. Kor knows the venue. I do not.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 156.</em> Tagged.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 156-216.</em> Frozen. Bleeding. Cannot move. The freeze duration in real time is three seconds. In subjective time it is approximately a season and a half. I watch Kor sprint away to chase the next-nearest target. I watch the next-nearest target also get tagged. I watch them die. I watch the bleed counter on my own HP bar tick down. I am reasonably sure I will die before the freeze ends.</p>
        <p><em>Tick 209.</em> I die. The freeze ends seven ticks later. The freeze ending is, in the rules, what would have allowed me to flee. The rules and the bleed are not, in practice, well-coordinated.</p>
        <p><em>Post-match.</em> Eleventh place. Coach says good job. Coach says we'll get them next year. Coach knows we will not get them next year.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Personal log, anonymous Panda Speedster, transcribed for the LMG broadcast cycle</div>
      </article>

      <article class="diary">
        <h3>The Sandbagged: A Knight's Notebook</h3>
        <div class="meta">Personal · LY 8 mid-season · author redacted at request</div>
        <p><em>Match 3, R2.</em> Felt sluggish all round. Couldn't reach a Brawler I should have outpaced. The kiting drill in practice goes 8 reps in 9 seconds. I went 6 in 9 today. Coach says it's the field. Coach has not seen the practice numbers from yesterday.</p>
        <p><em>Match 3, post.</em> The trainer logged my SPD as 4.2. The roster card says 4.5. The trainer says the difference is "within margin." The margin is, by the trainer's own definition, plus or minus 0.1. The difference is three margins.</p>
        <p><em>Match 4, pre.</em> I asked the trainer to weigh me. I weighed normal. I asked her to test the springs in my boots. The springs tested normal. I asked her to test the kiting drill again. She refused, on the grounds that the test would tire me out before the match. I performed the test anyway, in the warm-up tunnel, on a stopwatch I trust. 8 reps in 9.4 seconds. Within margin. Barely.</p>
        <p><em>Match 4, R3.</em> Outpaced a Sniper I had no business outpacing. Got the kill. Streak of two. Felt like myself. The trainer says it's the new boots. The boots are old. The boots have been old for a year and a half. The trainer is not lying — the trainer believes what she is saying. The trainer has been told to believe it.</p>
        <p><em>Match 5, R1.</em> Sluggish again. The kiting drill ran 6 in 9. Same gap as Match 3. Same opponent in the bracket as Match 3. The opposing fanbase is large and well-monied. I am not naming them. The Wing Standard ran a column on this last week. Some of you read it.</p>
        <p><em>Match 5, post.</em> I lost the duel I should have won. I am not going to say the word. The word is a thing my coach says lawyers will get involved over. The word is a thing my fanbase says in the comments under my match clips. The word is also probably true. I will not pursue this further. I cannot prove it. The League cannot prove it. The trainer cannot prove it. The buyer is anonymous. The system is designed this way. The system is not, by design, going to tell me when this happened or stops happening.</p>
        <p><em>Off-day.</em> I went home. My partner asked how the season was going. I told her about the kiting drill numbers. She asked what I wanted to do about it. I told her I wanted to train as if it kept happening, and play as if it didn't, and not think about it the rest of the time. She said: "That's the whole job." She was right. The whole job, this season, has gotten quietly harder.</p>
        <p><em>Match 6, R1.</em> Won the duel. 8 reps in 9. The trainer smiled. I did not.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Anonymous knight's notebook, partial, submitted to Foundation archives under Knight Privacy Provision 4-c</div>
      </article>

      <article class="diary">
        <h3>A Fan's Notebook</h3>
        <div class="meta">A diary · season-by-season notebook · longtime BVR fan, anonymous, Boston · LY 1–LY 8</div>
        <p><em>S1.</em> Watched the inaugural at Brewer's Reach. Cold day. Sat in the third row from the lobster booth. Saw Bristlebeard die. Cried. Did not tell anyone I cried. Resolved to never miss a match.</p>
        <p><em>S2.</em> Did not miss a match. Watched some on the broadcast — the camera was a stationary mount and the framing was the same every match. I learned to ignore the framing and watch the knights. The Wolves arrived. The Wolves bowed once, after a kill, and people in the stands argued about whether they meant it. I thought they meant it. The bow has stuck.</p>
        <p><em>S3.</em> Tomik fumbled the grenade. I was at Hardwood Hall on a road trip with my wife. Watched the round on her brother's portable broadcast set. The fumble was visible because the camera was wide. The fumble was unmistakable. The Sharks retired the number. My wife asked if Tomik had a family. I did not know. I have since learned. I send a card to Tomik's family on the anniversary, every year, anonymously. They have never written back. I do not need them to.</p>
        <p><em>S4.</em> Brask Ironclaw III died in Phoenix. His father coached. I had been watching Brask since his rookie year. I do not have words about Brask. I went to the funeral. I sat in the back. The lobster vendor was there. I do not know how she got to Phoenix.</p>
        <p><em>S5.</em> The Power Core was introduced. The first time it activated I did not understand what was happening. I do not know if I understand now. The Beavers' new Bristlebeard died in the season opener. I had hoped he would not. I cried again. I told my wife this time. She nodded.</p>
        <p><em>S6.</em> Pawington Cup. Mira's accidental flag-carry. The League formalized the event the same year. I bought a small embroidered kerchief at the concession stand and have it framed at home. The kerchief is the wrong color (Otter green; the original was indeterminate riverbank cloth). I do not care. The kerchief is the kerchief.</p>
        <p><em>S6 (continued).</em> The broadcast camera started moving this year. Treadwell's experiment at Hardwood Hall. The first dynamic shot felt strange. By the third match I had stopped noticing. By the tenth match I noticed when it didn't. The camera, when it works, is invisible. The camera, when it doesn't work, is the only thing on the screen.</p>
        <p><em>S7.</em> Crash Wartooth died. The Crash Rule was enacted. Hallowtide Tag became official. I did not watch the first Hallowtide live — I had a cold and stayed home. I watched the broadcast the next day. Kor Wartooth's run was the most beautiful sustained piece of athletic motion I have ever seen. I have watched the recording fourteen times. I will watch it again next Hallowtide.</p>
        <p><em>S8.</em> Hester Quillstrike died at Helena Glacier Rink. Sundip Hootington Jr. died at Reno tying her record. Hester's Rifle is missing. The Power Core has been patched. The field has been widened. The Field-Widening is, by Treadwell's framing, a camera operator's blessing — the camera has more to do, the knights have more room to move, the openings of rounds are watchable for the first time. I agree with Treadwell. I do not, however, like that I had to be told the openings were not watchable before. I had been watching the openings for eight years. I had thought I was watching something. I had been watching something. Treadwell is also right that I am watching something better now. Both can be true.</p>
        <p><em>S8 (continued).</em> The Beavers' new Bristlebeard — the Third — has not died. He is twenty-two and plays defense and the Beavers' fanbase is split about him. I am one of the Beavers' fanbase. I am split too. I want him to live. I want the pattern to be unbroken because the pattern is part of why I started watching. I want the pattern to be broken because the pattern has cost the Beavers eight Tanks and three families. Bjorn does not need to know what I want. Bjorn is doing his job.</p>
        <p>I will be at Brewer's Reach next match. The lobster vendor will be there. The camera will move. The Beavers will play. Bjorn will, gods willing, walk off the field at the end. I will be in the third row from the lobster booth. The seat is mine, by usage. I have never paid for it. The vendor knows me by name. She has never charged me for the bisque. I have never asked for the bisque. We have an understanding.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Anonymous BVR season-pass holder, notebook entries condensed and anonymized for League Library accession</div>
      </article>

      <article class="diary">
        <h3>Reporter's Notebook (TF)</h3>
        <div class="meta">A diary · Featherquill working notes, half-redacted · LY 8 mid-season</div>
        <p><em>Day 1.</em> Editor wants 600 words on the field-widening for Quarterly. Asked me to be opinionated. I am, by training, opinionated. The deadline is fine. The reporting is the harder part. The Rules Committee will not return calls. They never return calls. I will draft from secondary sources and re-interview when they break. The bulletin language is unusually plain. They are embarrassed. People are reliable when they are embarrassed.</p>
        <p><em>Day 2.</em> Talked to two coaches. Wolves' staff are upset. Otters' staff are euphoric. Talked to one Sniper anonymously — said she had not realized how often she had been firing from spawn until she watched her own film, and that the realization was uncomfortable. She said the wider field "feels like the sport waking up after a long nap." I will not use this quote on the record. It is too good and she will be punished for it. I will paraphrase.</p>
        <p><em>Day 4.</em> The Cultural Office's Reno paper leaked. Junior columnist at this paper got it. I did not get it. I am not bitter. I am, however, going to do my own draft of the Reno piece for next quarter, because the leaked version is more polish than reporting and the actual files have more in them. The intern who leaked it is a friend of an old friend. I will not name them in print but I will buy them a sandwich.</p>
        <p><em>Day 5.</em> Filed the field-widening column. Editor cut my line about "tactical phase that had been eroding." Said it was preachy. Editor was right. Replaced with: "the rounds are now slower at the start, and that is a thing the rounds were missing, and which the new rounds have." Editor said this was worse. I disagreed. I disagreed in writing. We compromised on neither version and wrote a third. The third is fine. I do not love the third. I love the second.</p>
        <p><em>Day 7.</em> Watched a full match on the new field. The opening sixty seconds of round one were a slow advance — both Snipers walking forward, neither in range, both visibly uncomfortable about not being in range. The Brawlers got there first. The Brawlers were excited about getting there first. The crowd noticed. The crowd was excited that the Brawlers were excited. I do not have an editorial position on this. I am writing it down because it felt good to watch.</p>
        <p><em>Day 9.</em> A knight emailed me. They had read the Quarterly column. They wrote: "thank you for not assuming we were already happy." I did not assume that. I do not, in general, assume that. The knight asked to remain anonymous. I have honored the request. I am writing this in a private notebook so the request stays honored.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Talia Featherquill, working notes, archived under journalist privilege</div>
      </article>
    </div>

        <h3>Match Log: Season 6 Final, BVR vs WLF (Boston, Brewer's Reach)</h3>
        <div class="meta">Official sportscaster transcript · 22:14 game time · partial</div>
        <p>22:14 — Bristlebeard the Second engages Crash Wartooth at center pitch. BST takes first swing.</p>
        <p>22:18 — Wartooth counters with Stun Baton. BST stunned, drops to one balloon.</p>
        <p>22:22 — Wartooth's three-Wolf rotation closes from the south side. Field is now five-vs-three in WLF's favor.</p>
        <p>22:31 — BST attempts to retrieve Plasma Mace from a fallen Beaver Brawler. BST stunned again. WLF rotation closes the kill.</p>
        <p>22:34 — Bristlebeard the Second falls. The Beavers begin a slow-motion retreat to the back zone.</p>
        <p>22:48 — A single Beaver Healer reaches the back zone. The Healer is alone.</p>
        <p>22:52 — The Wolves begin their classic pack engagement. Five knights converge on the lone Healer.</p>
        <p>22:54 — The Beaver Healer drops a Health Pack at her own feet. She heals to full balloons. The Wolves arrive.</p>
        <p>22:55 — The Healer pops her own Power Cell. The Healer enters berserk mode. The Wolves engage anyway.</p>
        <p>23:11 — The Healer is the last Beaver standing. The Wolves are at three knights. The crowd is on its feet. This is the longest "last knight" sequence in BVR history.</p>
        <p>23:34 — The Healer falls to a Wolf Sniper firing from the back of the stadium. The Wolves win.</p>
        <p>23:36 — The Wolves do not celebrate. They walk to the Healer's body. They form a circle. The Wolf captain bows. The crowd is silent. This will later be called "The Bow."</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Boston Sportscaster Booth, Season 6 final</div>
      </article>

      <article class="log">
        <h3>Match Log: Season 8 QF3, OWL vs FRG (Reno Open Court)</h3>
        <div class="meta">Note: this match was played on a tennis court, which neither team practices on</div>
        <p>00:34 — Both teams enter the court. Neither team is sure where the spawn lines are.</p>
        <p>00:48 — A Frog speedster slides on the clay surface and rolls into the net. The Frog comes up with the net wrapped around the Frog's head. The match resumes anyway.</p>
        <p>01:12 — Sundip Hootington Jr. (Owl Sniper) sets up at the back service line. Sundip is on his last career month. Sundip does not know it.</p>
        <p>02:48 — A Frog Grenadier throws a frag grenade. The grenade lands on the singles sideline. The grenade rolls along the sideline for nineteen feet without losing pace, hits a Frog Healer, and detonates.</p>
        <p>02:52 — The Frog Healer is, against expectation, fine. The Frog Healer's heal beam, on the other hand, has been knocked into a horizontal arc that points only at the south wall. The Frog Healer cannot heal her teammates for the rest of the match.</p>
        <p>04:03 — Power Core spawns at center court. Both teams ignore it.</p>
        <p>04:31 — Power Core is finally captured by an Owl Brawler who needed a place to lean.</p>
        <p>06:14 — Sundip Hootington Jr. is, by this point, the only Owl on the field. He has 9 kills. The Frogs have one survivor: a Speedster.</p>
        <p>06:22 — The Frog Speedster runs the clay. Sundip tracks. The clay is slick. The Speedster is not slick. The Speedster falls.</p>
        <p>06:23 — Sundip fires the kill shot. He is on the back service line. The shot is a 412-pace railshot. It is the longest kill shot in League history. It will stand for three full seasons.</p>
        <p>06:24 — Sundip walks to the Power Core to celebrate. Sundip <em>climbs onto</em> the Power Core to celebrate. Sundip's celebration is interrupted by the Power Core's overload sequence, which has not been seen in eight seasons. Sundip does not survive the celebration.</p>
        <p>06:24 — The match ends in an Owl win. The match ends in Sundip Hootington Jr.'s permadeath. Both things are true. The crowd is unsure how to react. The Frog Speedster, in fairness, also dies of the overload, which gives the Frogs partial credit on the post-mortem stats.</p>
        <div class="signoff">— Reno Sportscaster Booth, edited for length</div>
      </article>
